## Crevice of Dusk

Crevices of Dusk were portals, gateways, or otherwise "gaps" between the Material Plane and the Shadowfell that occurred throughout Neverwinter and its Shadowfell twin, Evernight.

Description
Crevices of Dusk were magical phenomena that connected a point in Neverwinter to the same point in Evernight, and vice versa. They could appear anywhere throughout Evernight but only appeared in places of darkness or shadow within Neverwinter. Under most circumstances, it was only possible to see or reach through a Crevice but not to travel through it. Even so, it was not unheard of for travelers to pass through one, whether intentionally, by accident, or after having been dragged through by a monster on the other side. Such Crevices were the primary source of food for the undead citizens of Evernight, namely by snatching away living citizens of Neverwinter.

While it was said that there were countless Crevices throughout Neverwinter and Evernight, they tended to open unpredictably and for only a short time before changing location randomly. In some cases, rituals or misfired magic might cause a latent Crevice to open. Although rare, there also existed stable and permanent Crevices of Dusk, which appeared as swirls of silvery-purple energy. Such Crevices usually occurred within catacombs, tombs, coffins, abandoned buildings, back alleyways, unused cellars, or even dark corners of inhabited homes.

History
As of the late 15 century DR, Crevices of Dusk were not widely known or understood in Neverwinter, and were commonly sources of ghost stories and urban myths relating to hauntings and mysterious disappearances. Some of Neverwinter's scholars (such as Eldon Keyward) were familiar with them and their nature, and were of course perturbed by their use. Within Evernight, indiscriminate use of the Crevices of Dusk was frowned upon, and stable Crevices were jealously guarded.

In the, Valindra Shadowmantle created a shadow crossing in Neverwinter's Neverdeath Graveyard, which served as a more reliable means of travel from the Material Plane to the Shadowfell than the unpredictable Crevices of Dusk.

Notable Crevices of Dusk
 Evernight's Corpse Market was well-known to be the site of many Crevices of Dusk, which were considered freely available for use by the hungry undead public.
 The Dolindar Tomb had a stable Crevice connecting Evernight's Graveyard to Neverwinter's Neverdeath Graveyard.
 A Crevice of Dusk located in Neverdeath's Pauper's Field could be activated using a special coffer possessed by the lich Zarifrax.

## Crested helmet

Crested helmets were an open-faced style of helmet found in the Realms.Though these helmets would more accurately be referred to as "Corinthian helmets" or "Hoplite helmets", such historical names are ill-fitting for the Realms. The name "crested helmet" is taken from the historical campaign sourcebook Age of Heroes, though due to the genericness of such a name it should not be assumed that any mention of a "crested helmet" in the Realms is referring to this style of helmet.

Description
These style of helmets covered most of the wearer's head and face, but left the center of the face open. They were distinguished from other helmets by a tufted crest across the helm's front axis. Some also sported flaps, which allowed the helmet's cheek pieces to be raised aside whenever a wearer was out of combat.

History
Sometime after the sundering of the Ordning in 1485 DR, the fire giant warlord Baron Krelas Ugarak assembled an army of fire giants, goblinoids, and human mercenaries to wage a campaign of conquest against Secomber, moving on to Daggerford and eventually Waterdeep. As part of this nefarious plan he was developing a new form of siege engine, known as inferno bombards. The entrance to the Molten Vault where they were being developed was guarded by a large contingent of goblinoids, with the hobgoblins all outfitted in blackened steel armor and crested helmets.As the hobgoblins in this adventure are described as wearing "armor resembling hoplites", it is assumed that the style of helmet described in the adventure is likewise referring to a Corinthian helmet.

## Crown of Lies

The Crown of Lies was an artifact that granted its wearer the ability to perfectly transform into any seen creature. Additionally, any lies spoken while in the new form would appear as truth to both mundane and magical senses.

Description
The crown was crafted from burnished and intertwined metal rods.

Powers
In order to attune with the Crown of Lies, its user should place it on their head and speak their heart's true desire.

The crown's wearer could assume the form and feel of any creature they'd seen, as long as the creature's size didn't deviate too far from the wearer's own size, generally within a range of half or double the wearer's size. No form of magical or mundane scrutiny would reveal details about the nature of the transformation, nor would it detect lies spoken as the new form. The crown would meld into the wearer's new form, thus rendering it undetectable. However, the wearer could choose to make the crown visible at will. The transformation would last until the wearer died, the attunement with the crown was broken, or the wearer used its powers to assume a new form.

The Crown of Lies would be permanently destroyed if the wearer was killed by the creature they were transformed into.

Notable Owners
 Kas, who found the crown in a hidden forge in the Shadowfell.

Crowell Tefoco was a caravan guard and one of the older children of Oliver Tefoco, the proprietor of the Beehive Goods and Wares general store in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Relationships
Crowell was a son to Oliver and Kroyce Tefoco, and had a number of siblings. Thegglis' brother Thegglis was a fellow caravan guard, and the youngest siblings Hemjax, Maggie, and others, still lived with Oliver and Kroyce as of the late 15 century DR.

History
In the, as the Cult of the Absolute threatened Baldur's Gate's safety, Thegglis and Crowell were stuck with their Seven Suns Trading Coster caravan at Boareskyr Bridge. The caravan's return to the gate was postponed due to the cult's threat and the city gates being closed. During their travels, the older caravan guards taught Thegglis and Crowell much of the trade, according to the letter home Thegglis penned for Crowell, who could not yet write.

## Crypt of Vers Never

History
The unassuming tomb was identified as the crypt of Vers Never around the, by Retrigal Bookbinder, a scholar working to validate Lord Neverember's claim of descent from Nasher Alagondar. As Vers was known to have settled in Waterdeep, Retrigal presumed that the crypt would be empty, however he hoped that it might still contain something of interest (such as records of Vers Never's lineage). After identifying the crypt, Retrigal sought to enlist the help of an adventurer to neutralize any undead near it.

## Crypt of the Moondark

The Crypt of the Moondark was the family crypt of the Moondark family in Westgate.

Structure

There was a sign posted outside of the crypt, until Elminster Aumar defaced it with a spell that made it crumble to dust. Guards could rest along one corner of the crypt, while the opposite path was lined with stones.

Interior
There were stacked stone coffers in a room inside, which had a funeral slab in the center. Older coffins had the likeness of the departed on their lids, while the newer coffins were plain, some even nameless.

Defenses
Inside, there were a number of traps, including door wards, a falling beam trap, and a number of jutting blades.

Outside, there were guards posted on a corner.

History
In the, about a hundred and sixty years after the Moondark family had all perished, Elminster entered the crypt and placed an enchanted wand, an everbright dagger that glowed upon command, and spell notes in the late Eaergladden Moondark's coffin.

Rumors & Legends
Elminster knew that Eaergladden Moondark was no wizard, and that he'd in fact died penniless, but counted on the fact that no one would know this if they came looking for hidden magic.

## Cream

Cream, sometimes referred to as whipping cream, was a fatty milk product found throughout the Realms and used in savory cooking as well as pastry making.

Description
Cream was a viscous white dairy product kept in jugs and whipped for use in cooking, giving bakeries and kitchens a pleasant aroma that often went hand-in-hand with the smell of blooming yeast. Pails of cream were sold to butteries, where it was churned into butter.

Usages
Waterdhavian meals sometimes included bowls of clotted cream and strawberries.

In the city of Neverwinter, whipped cream was used to offset kaeth's bitter taste. It was also used as a thickener in a puréed potato soup called cawl cennin.

People of Baldur's Gate used whipping cream as an ingredient in making fiddlehead soup.

Mushrooms in cream was a specialty of Swordsfall House in Silverymoon.

Cream from the Moonshae Isles was a staple of a hearty morningfeast: eggs, bacon, and fresh cream. Similarly, a morningfeast in Suzail, the capital of Cormyr, included sliced fruit in cream. Lavish morningfeasts in the city of Arrabar in Chondath included fresh cream, chilled and whipped to be smothered over bread.

Some desserts made with cream included a bowl made out of chilled chocolate, filled with sweet cherries and cream—a delicacy from Shadowdale in the Dalelands. Cormyrean desserts included whipped mousse made with Maztican chocolate and almond liqueur, topped with whipped cream.

Outside of the Prime Material plane, cream was available in Finder Wyvernspur's realm of Fermata in Arborea.

A cream of long years was a magical item that countered aging and preserved undead.

The cream was thick and lumpy. Jars of it normally contained six applications.

When applied to the skin of a living person, cream of long years reduced their age by one year overnight. Multiple applications in the same 24-hour period did not stack. When applied to the skin of a corporeal undead, it stopped them from rotting for three months.

History
In the, the lich Ezzat had recently developed the recipe for such a cream. He used it to preserve himself.

## Crimson Draughts

Crimson Draughts was an alchemist's shop located on Stormshore Street in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate across from Beehive Goods and Wares general store in the late 15 century DR. It was run by Araj Oblodra.

Location
Crimson Draughts was a single-storied but spacious building with its main floor reserved for the business and alchemical experiments done in the basement. Both shops, Draughts and Beehive Goods, were somewhere roughly southeast of The Blushing Mermaid in the Lower City.

Services
Some of the goods offered at the Draughts were unreliable at best. One of Araj's customers penned an angry letter to the shop, claiming that after imbibing one of her sanguineous potions, their limbs began moving on their own accord and their blood boiled like oil. Lastly, they were unable to sleep for a tenday thanks to the voices in their head.

Crimson Draughts carried some magic items and potions, as well as volatile substances: smokepowder bombs, a hat of storm scion's power, a thunderskin cloak, a robe of exquisite focus, rings of free action, a risky ring, a pair of boots of arcane bolstering, and a circlet of hunting. Among the shop's more notable concoctions was sanguine explosive, a potion of everlasting vigor, as well as various sanguineous distillates of humanoid blood that temporarily shared various races' properties with the imbibers.

History
By the, Crimson Draughts was run by an eccentric and ambitious drow Araj Oblodra, an alchemist and trader in blood and the sanguinous arts. She was known to do business with the dread Cult of the Absolute and operated the shop in Baldur's Gate with some success. By the time the Absolute crisis began to shake the Gate, Crimson Draughts had had several months' worth of customer complaints, prompting an investigation by the Flaming Fists. An official investigation was opened in accordance with the Public Health Act and the Civilian Protection Order, and a notification was mailed to the shop. Araj Oblodra was ordered not to interfere with the investigation and not to leave the city or she would be held in contempt of the Council of Four.

Araj Oblodra might have had an opportunity to use her shop's laboratory to develop special and deadly types of potions using the blood of the Absolute Adversaries, if the heroes were inclined to share their life-giving substance. If her research was successful, Araj could've left the Gate for her House's home in Menzoberranzan to bring House Oblodra into good graces with the new sanguineous magics. The fate of Crimson Draughts remains unknown.

## Crown of Salt (adventure)

Crown of Salt is an adventure module set in the Forgotten Realms using the Dungeons & Dragons 2-edition ruleset. It is part of the Adventurer's Guild series of adventures published for the Adventurer's Guild Retail Play Program. It promotes both the Sea of Fallen Stars and Of Ships and the Sea sourcebooks. It starts and concludes in Selgaunt but is focused on the Sea of Fallen Stars, and is an adventure designed for four to six 5- to 6-level characters.

Summary
Andeth Ilchammar sends a rival of his', Sturge Whitsunday, in a doomed mission to find Myth Nantar so that the city of Sembia can sign a treaty with them for defense against pirates. Once they find Myth Nantar against all odds, authorities in the underwater city require them to retrieve the Crown of Salt from Agenais before they accept any treaties.

## Create object

Create object was a minor psionic devotion of the psychokinesis discipline that allowed a psionicist to assemble, to a limited degree, a material object from the air and potentially other material around them.

Effects
Items formed by create object could be of any color, shape, and texture that the psionicist desired. However, the minor devotion had certain limitations to size and weight that an object could take. An object couldn't weigh any more than  and had to be able to fit within either a sphere no more than  in diameter, within a cylinder no more than  in length and  in diameter, or a cylinder no more than  in height and  in diameter.

A psionicist could only draw from material within a  radius of them when using create object, though the materials they chose for the construction of their object could be rearranged or restructured if they so happened to have molecular rearrangement. For example, the combination of these two powers could allow a psionicist to form diamonds out of coal dust or a sword from mere iron ore.

Any matter formed from create object was usually temporary, existing so long as the psionicist focused upon it. When a psionicist ceased to put mental energy towards maintaining an object, it would fall apart into its constituent parts.

On the rare instance when a psionicist botched using create object, the item they created would contain some sort of flaw. If the use of create object outright backfired, one of the psionicist's personal belongings would be disintegrated.

Prerequisite Powers
In order to learn this power, a psion first had to possess the minor devotion telekinesis.

Notable Users
Races & Sentient Creatures
 Ki-rin, a race of celestial creatures with psionic powers.
 Lythlyx, a strange psionic race of eel-like creatures.

## Cryptkey Facilitations

Cryptkey Facilitations was a business in the Lower Trade Lanes of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
Cryptkey Facilitations was a two-story establishment with an expansive basement, operating in fair condition. The building was heavily guarded by undead creatures, including a formidable skull-headed ettin zombie and a swarm of crawling claws.

Interior
To preserve their inventory of corpses prior to reanimation, Cryptkey Facilitations utilized a cool basement and sub-basement, extending the storage space beneath its modest two-story exterior.

Services
Cryptkey Facilitations primary service lay in the procurement and preparation of corpses, which they carefully stitched and mended as necessary to supply high-quality bodies for reanimation. Utilizing spells such as Nulathoe's ninemen and preserve dead, the organization ensured that each corpse was in optimal condition for necromantic purposes. They maintained close associations with figures like Mhaug and Dalagor the Cold.

In addition to providing bodies for reanimation, Cryptkey Facilitations offered a highly specialized, though costly, exhumation service. For those with the means to pay, the organization retrieved the remains of specific individuals—often heroes or well-known figures—from Waterdeep's City of the Dead. Access to these remains was enabled by a network of concealed passages and gates, allowing for clandestine entry and egress. However, due to the expense involved and the high demand for cheap labor in Skullport, this service was infrequently requested. The cost of recovering such a body varied widely, from hundreds to thousands of gold pieces, influenced by the nature of the burial and circumstances surrounding the individual's death.

Inhabitants
Cryptkey Facilitations was run by a group of thieves and necromancers who profited by plundering crypts and graves within the City of the Dead. The organization's personnel typically numbered around twelve at any given time, each member wearing stylized masks to obscure their identities and maintaining a stoic silence. Circa 1370 DR, the operation was led by Jasmar'n Shadewidow, a reserved and introspective woman of notable necromantic skill.

References

## Crowing Cockatrice

The Crowing Cockatrice was an inn in the Lower Trade Lanes of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
The Crowing Cockatrice was a modest two-story building in squalid condition.

Services
Rooms at the Crowing Cockatrice were perpetually available. Each room contains only a sea chest and a straw-filled mattress, and patrons could expect bedbugs as an unwelcome but freely provided amenity. Prices for a private room were negotiable, with the typical rate being four silver pieces per night.

The inn's offerings were basic at best. With ingredients scavenged from the Fish Market, local Underdark fungi, and dubious tubers, patrons were served a barely palatable stew at minimal cost: one copper piece for a cup and two for a bowl. Despite the poor quality of the food, the inn’s selection of ale and beer was passable, with a tankard priced at approximately two silver pieces.

History
The inn was owned by "Slop" Gallowshawk, a former scullery cook. The inn has a unique history shaped by both misfortune and unlikely survival. Circa 1350 DR, Slop survived a shipwreck and was swept through the treacherous channels and locks from the South Seacaves, ultimately arriving in Skullport with a chest containing his captain's private treasure. Transformed from a cook to a wealthy man, Slop aspired to establish the Crowing Cockatrice as one of the port’s premier inns. However, lacking any real business acumen, he squandered his newfound wealth within a decade, leaving the inn in its current dilapidated state.

Inhabitants
The most remarkable feature of the inn was Setana, a watchghost who served as an efficient, if spectral, barmaid. Newcomers were often startled by trays floating through the air and empty tankards refilling on their own, all owing to Setana's silent service. Her origin remained a mystery; when asked, Slop simply remarked that she "came with the place." The inn also occasionally hosted Shaun Taunador, an illithid potion-seller, who used the Crowing Cockatrice for brief stays during his visits to Skullport.

## Crow's Foot

Crow's Foot was a hamlet at the foot of the city of Ravens Bluff, on the eastern shores of the Dragon Reach.

Location
The hamlet was situated outside of the protective stone walls of the city, west of its Foreign District. The only road that connected it to the city was the Front.The hamlet is not depicted in the map of Ravens Bluff in the Forgotten Realms Interactive Atlas, but the source is cited here for the sake of identifying the road.

Description
Most of the buildings in the hamlet were built in an architectural style known on some worlds as "Tudor". Many were built of stucco, some simple stone, and thatched roofs were quite prominent. Narrow cart paths passed for streets in the hamlet and no torches were set out to illuminate them during the night. During the evening one could see the light of hearthfires and candles from the many windows of residents.

It was not uncommon to hear conch shells being blown during the night, either as a religious act towards Istishia during nights when the Veil was up or out of a local belief that doing so would ward off monsters.

Law & Order
The City Watch and Night Watch of Ravens Bluff oversaw the hamlet's law enforcement, though things were usually quite peaceful in Crow's Foot.

History
On a foggy night around the spring or fall season of the, a group of newly minted Night Watchmen patrolled the hamlet and had a myriad of encounters. First they saved a middle-aged woman, who was on her to slop the two pigs she kept in her backyard, from three mangy dogs. They then met the vampire hunter Jack Harkness and accompanied him to the Red Sail Inn, where they ran off four human hooligans that were posing as members of the Night Watch in an effort to shake the tavernkeep down for protection money. As they tried to leave Crow's Foot to patrol Old Town they investigated a fireball and tremendous pyrotechnic display in the nightsky that were the cause of two wizards, Modo and Yvas, arguing over which of them had cast the spell that slayed a blue dragon back in their adventuring days.

In the, a group of fire worshipers emerged in Crow's FootInferno in the Living City makes some references to the events of Nightwatch in the Living City, referring to them as having happened four years prior. As that module has been estimated to be set in 1355 DR, that would place the events of Inferno in 1359 DR. called the Cult of Red Ruin and established a temple close to the Red Sail Inn. Sometime later that year the cult found and trapped within an iron flask a pyre elemental. Over the next two weeks the cultists caused several fires in Ravens Bluff, until one day they caused a significant wave of fires in Crow's Foot, Old Town, and the Warehouse District.

Notable Locations
 Raven's Eye, formerly known as the Red Sail Inn, was an inn and tavern close to the outer wall. It was one of two taverns in the hamlet,Nightwatch in the Living City states there are at least two or more taverns in Crow's Foot, though the name of this other tavern (or taverns) is never given. and most residents chose to flock to it instead of making the trek down to Old Town.
 Temple of Red Ruin, a marble temple near the Raven's Eye that was operated by the Cult of Red Ruin.

Inhabitants
The hamlet was inhabited by many families, as well as merchants that worked in Old Town.

Notable Inhabitants
 Jack Harker, an old and semi-retired vampire hunter.
 Modo, a conjurer that ran an alchemical store in Old Town.
 Thomas and T.J., an orphaned child and his grandfather that lived in a cottage in Crow's Foot in the mid-14 century DR.
 Yvas, an illusionist that ran a small scribe shop in Old Town.

## Crimes of the Moonmaiden

Crimes of the Moonmaiden was a Sharran text, penned in or before the 15 century DR by The Anti-Lunar League.

Content
The Crimes of the Moonmaiden scroll was a work of heresy aimed to undermine the Selûnite teachings in favor or the Sharran worship. The work claimed that Selûne would make her worshipers to think that their husband was a werewolf if he only forgot to shave. The scroll also stated that Selûne was always changing, making it hard for the painters to capture her image. The Anti-Lunar League also claimed the tides on Selûne, as well as claimed that the moon's association with femmininity brought forth countless examples of derivative poetry.

History
In the, copies of the Crimes of the Moonmaiden scrolls were found within the library of the Cloister of Sombre Embrace, a secret Sharran sect underneath the Lower City of Baldur's Gate, as well as sold by Nansi Gretta in her Lower City bookstore, The Bibliophile.

## Creation mantle

The Creation mantle was a psionic mantle that granted ardents and divine minds an array of psionics powers and abilities derived from the universal concept or philosophy of creation, themed around creating objects.

Deities

Abilities
Those who followed the creation mantle obtained a greater mastery over shaping ectoplasm, allowing them to create a variation of astral construct that was distinct in both appearance and abilities, as well as the potential to create other variations in the future.In mechanical terms, the Creation mantle granted "...the Ectopic Form feat (page 50) as a bonus feat..." and allowed the player to "...learn the form of your choice." Additionally, the psychic aura exuded by divine minds of the creation mantle had a restorative effect on constructs. The possible variations of astral constructs were as followed:

 Agile Loper: A sleek quadruped form, similar in appearance to a bariaur, making the astral construct faster and more agile.Though not explicitly referred to as a "bariaur" appearance, the description of "centaurlike quadrupeds with pale fur" and "ramlike horns on their foreheads" heavily implies such.
 Alabaster Aerial: A silvery-white humanoid form with clawed feet and wings in place of arms.
 Amber Tunneler: An amber-colored buglike form, with a wide body and numerous short legs that walked low to the ground, making the astral construct a gifted burrower.
 Anathemic Carapace: An oversized red scarab form with fiery eyes. The astral construct unleashed a blast of psychic energy upon death. Alternatively, the manifester could remotely detonate the construct.
 Astral Aquan: A silvery-blue eel-like form, with a pair of long tentacles protruding from their mouth. The astral construct became a gifted swimmer, capable of pummeling foes with its tentacles.
 Ebony Stinger: A shiny black arachnid form with a hooked tail. The astral construct gained the power to deliver a minor poison with the very sharp barb on their tail.
 Emerald Gyre: A green apelike form with a third arm in the middle of its back, making the construct a better grappler.
 Iridescent Serpent: A distinct serpentine form with a clubbed tail, imbued with an energy type of the manifester's choice–acid, cold, electricity, or fire. The astral construct gained minute resistance to that energy type and their attacks became imbued with it.

Powers
Novice manifesters gained the astral construct power that the Creation mantle's unique ability necessitated, as well as the power to create cloth and wooden items with psionic minor creation.

Manifesters of some experience could transform raw materials into objects with psionic fabricate. As a manifester progressed further they gained improved versions of the former two powers, psionic major creation and greater psionic fabricate. Masters of this mantle reached the pinnacle of creation, becoming able to create enduring objects with true creation.

## Crèche Y'llek egg

The Crèche Y'llek egg was a githyanki egg laid in Crèche Y'llek, though it had trouble hatching. Xan or Ptaris were the names possibly given to it were it to be taken away from the Crèche, depending on whose custody it was in upon hatching.

History
In the, Xan's egg was laid in Crèche Y'llek, a githyanki stronghold in the Western Heartlands, within the remains of Rosymorn Monastery. The eggs within the hatchery were destined to become warriors in service to Vlaakith CLVII. However, Xan's egg fell behind its peers and failed to hatch on time. Varsh Ko'kuu, the overseer of the hatchery, was determined to ensure the egg's survival, but under increasing pressure from Kith'rak Therezzyn, he reluctantly considered terminating it.

That year, the Absolute Adversaries may have arrived at the crèche seeking a cure for their illithid tadpoles using the zaith'isk.

Appendix
Gameplay

In gameplay of Baldur's Gate III, the unhatched Crèche Y'llek egg is requested to be retrieved by Lady Esther, an adventurer working for the Society of Brilliance. If the egg were to be entrusted to Esther, she would then pass it along to Havkelaag of the Society to be raised away from the cruel gith upbringing in an experiment intended prove that one's upbringing is more important than one's species. The egg would then be hatched and brought to adulthood via magic, named Ptaris, after the Bahamutan Ptarian Code. As Ptaris grew, his mind would become warped, treating the doctrine zealously and believing himself to be a paragon of good. Eventually, the doctrine would convince Ptaris that the Society was evil, leading him to murder every member of the chapter, including his adopted father and Lady Esther. If Ptaris were to be confronted by the Absolute Adversaries following the murders, he could be killed in battle. Alternatively, he could be convinced of the error of his ways, making the young gith inconsolable with grief and regret.

According to another possibility, the githyanki warrior Lae'zel may be disturbed by the treatment of the unhatched egg and convince Ko'kuu to release the egg into her care to prevent its destruction. Initially wary, Lae'zel would grow increasingly protective of the egg over time. Six months after the game's end, the egg would hatch under Lae'zel's care. She names the hatchling Xan, meaning "freedom" in the Gith language, reflecting her hope that he would one day choose his own path. Alternatively, Lae'zel raises him with the additional goal of preparing him to lead a future githyanki resistance against Vlaakith in the name of The Prince of the Comet. These events are unconfirmed, but the name is used here for convenience.

If the egg ends up never being entrusted to Lady Esther but taken from Crèche Y'llek and not given to Lae'zel, or if Lae'zel chooses to follow Vlaakith CLVII, the egg hatches on its own and a feral githyanki child is seen crawling around the Elfsong Tavern after the Absolute crisis is over.

Lastly, if the egg is left within Crèche Y'llek, it is likely eventually destroyed by the githyanki or by Rosymorn Monastery's destruction.

Services
The shop offered a variety of crudely shaped ceramic and clay vessels, basins, and urns, all produced with minimal concern for aesthetics. Despite their unattractive appearance, these items remained surprisingly functional and were sold at inexpensive prices.

Inhabitants
Crack'd Pots was owned by Estellia Vizen, who claimed to have been born in Skullport nearly a century ago. Her advanced age, coupled with her clay-caked demeanor, lent some credence to her assertion. ## Crisis of breath

Crisis of breath was a rare psionic devotion of the telepathy discipline, and its compulsion subdiscipline. Originally developed by mind flayers, it allowed a manifester to disrupt a creature's autonomic breathing cycle.

Effects
A creature targeted by crisis of breath had autonomic failure induced in their mind. This disruption of the target's nervous system compelled them to expel the air currently stored within their lungs and disabled those organs' systematic functioning, forcing the target to consciously control their breathing. This did not prevent the afflicted from undertaking other actions, but failure to focus on breathing could lead the target to blacking out from lack of oxygen.

The version originally developed by mind flayers was a persistent affliction, lasting thirty days or more and necessitating some extraordinary means of healing to recover from sooner, while the one later used by psionic users of other races had a limited duration. The means of healing included heal, psychic surgery, restoration, and their equivalents. Cure disease had no affect.

As the original version had a longer-lasting effect, those afflicted by crisis of breath were incapable of sleeping, as a lapse in concentration could risk their life. This lack of sleep in turn would cause a natural degeneration in the afflicted's health, increased irritability, and ever-increasing incidences of hallucinating.

Originally this power had a maximum targeting range of . Around the late 14 century DR, the version used by manifesters of other races could target creatures from  away, though depending upon how skilled the manifester was this range was extended in increments of .

Displays
When used this power manifested an auditory display.

Users
This power was knowable by psion telepaths and mind flayers, who originally entrusted elder brains with safeguarding the knowledge of it.

Known/Notable Users
 Lugribossk, a proxy of the mind flayer god Ilsensine.
 The Mind Shard of Pandorym, the imprisoned form of the eponymous elder evil.
 Saryndalaghlothtor, an adult crystal dragon.
 Zelia, a yuan-ti telepath, who once used crisis of breath on the young psion Arvin.

## Crab

Crab was a collective name given to a number of species of crustaceans found in the Realms and often used in culinary pursuits. There existed mundane and giant crabs, as well as saltwater and freshwater varieties.

Description
Crabs were foul-tempered crustaceans encased in shells with its colors differing depending on the species. They had powerful pincer claws that, depending on the crab's side, could open an adventurer's armor like a tin can. They were also known to be able to become one's familiar or an animal companion, while giant crabs could be dominated through magic and turned into fine mounts.

 Bigclaw, a species of giant blue-shelled crab, larger than river snipper crab, that inhabited the Soshenstar River in Chult.
 Blue king crab, one of the biggest and most expensive species of crab from the Frozenfar.
 Devil crab, an edible species of crab eaten in the Vast.
 Dikanie, a species of orange  crab from Kara-Tur.
 Emperor crab, a lesser but dangerous species of king crab from the Sea of Moving Ice with succulent meat.
 Finialian's crab, a species of brightly-colored crab found in Scardale.
 Flameshell crab, a species of red-shelled crab.
 Giant crabs were a common  long danger found in the Underdark and an edible prey to stronger creatures, svirfneblins, and adventurers. They were also commonly found in the Sea of Fallen Stars. There existed giant crab ghosts in the eastern lands of Kara-Tur.
 Hermit crab, a species of crap that used discarded shells, found in the Sea of Swords and Waterdeep.
 Hulking crab, a species of crab bigger than the giant crab.
 Purple marsh crab, a species of freshwater river crab from Chult.
 River snipper, a species of giant brown-shelled crabs that inhabited the Soshenstar River in Chult.
 Sand crab, a species of crab pests.
 Siege crab, a titanic crab and a living siege machine created in the time of the Tenth Serôs War.
 Soft-shelled crab, various species of edible crabs.
 White helmet, an anemic-colored cave-dwelling crab from the Sea of Moving Ice.
 Wooly crab, a species of the Sea of Moving Ice crab with wool-like spines on their shells.
 Yeti crab, a species of large deep-sea saltwater crab from the Sea of Moving Ice found near seafloor thermal vents.

Ecology
Crabs were commonly cooked across the Realms. In Calimshan, crabs were served boiled and stuffed.

In Cormyr, soft-shelled crabs were breaded and fried, served dusted in sugar. Another Coemyte treat was Marsemban tarts that contained diced salmon and crab.

Along the Dragon Coast, clams and crabs were abundant in Westgate, where the city's poor descended on the beaches to dig for their next meals.

In the Dalelands, freshwater crabs were caught for food in Archendale, along the shores of the River Arkhen.

On the Sword Coast, crab meat was used to make crab cakes, like specialty dish of the Elfsong Tavern in Baldur's Gate.

In the Frozenfar, numerous crab species inhabited the Sea of Moving Ice, but smaller crab was fished out of Maer Dualdon and used as a food source by the locals. The meat was nutritious but quite bland in flavor. The region's crabs consumed serpent stars and fishers sometimes used them as crab fishing bait. The crab fishers of the Ten Towns used pots to trap the critters.

Sharkey's Bar & Grill of Ravens Bluff in The Vast sold live-caught crabs, and served cooked giant crab's legs or whole giant crabs. Crabs were also the specialty of the Fresh Fish shop and The Lambent Eye, where crabs were fried in lemon beer. And Fish Tails served fishcakes made with various sea-critters, including crab. And in the nearby Mossbridges, the Golden Goblet Inn had stuffed devil crab on the menu.

Notable Crabs

 Karkinos, a massive and powerful legendary bigclaw crab who terrorized Chult in the late 15 century DR.
 Suratuk's giant red crab, a creation of an eccentric Calishite artist who permanently dyed and pained companion and mount animals in the late 15 century DR.
 Suratuk's giant sunset crab, another victim of Suratuk's paintbrush.

Such crucibles did not operate underwater.

## Crane Point

Crane Point was a promontory stretching into Ulls lake in the Highlands of the island of Gwynneth in the Moonshae Isles. It was likely named for the local rainbow cranes.

Geography
It was a spit of land that was most directly accessible only during low tide, when a strip of sand became traversable along the edges of Ulls.

History
During the time of the human kingdom of Corwell, Crane Point was the site of a well-defended temple. After the takeover of Gwynneth by the fey of Sarifal, the temple and its surrounding defenses fell into ruin. Instead, Crane Point came to be the site of hunting forays. It featured stables for horses and an open field in which human slaves were made to assemble and disassemble pavilions for the enjoyment of the fey, who would hunt, drink, listen to music, and gawk at the cranes around the ruined temple.

In the late 1460s DR, Crane Point was the site of a battle between drow mercenaries in service to the ruling leShay and a group of traitors attempting to smuggle a young leShay princess, Amaranth, out of the realm. While Amaranth was somewhat successfully handed off to a hippogriff rider, all of those who had struggled to help her escape, notably the elf Valeanne and the dragonborn Shamasar and his underlings, all perished. Ten years later, the failure of the drow at Crane Point continued to be held over their heads by the leShay.

## Cragwardens

The cragwardens were defenders of the ancient dwarven stronghold of Highhome on Gwynneth in the Moonshae Isles. These dwarves were accordingly known by the title of Cragwarden.

History
Following the return of the fey realm of Sarifal to Gwynneth in the, evil fey would subsequently attack the local dwarves, forcing them to all but abandon the island and flee through the Dwarvenhome Tunnels. By the late 15 century DR, only a small retinue of cragwardens remained in Highhome as the last guardians of their ancient fastness.

Notable Members
 Finellen, the venerable leader of the cragwardens of Highhome as of the .
 Samiel Tussin, a cragwarden who traveled to Itzcala in the mid-to-late-14 century DR.

## Crimson crystal golem

Crimson crystal golems were a unique variety of golems created to celebrate the Protector's Jubilee, an anniversary celebration of the city's rebirth, hosted in the late 15 century DR in Neverwinter, the Jewel of the North.

Description
Crimson crystal golems were, as it was evident from the name, carved of red crystal and resembled ceremonial armor sets created for the same celebration for the city's many adventurers.

Combat
These constructs were crafted purely for ceremonial use, to attend Neverwintran galas and banquets. However, they also had some limited use in battle. Their body could be shattered in battle, dealing devastating damage to the owner's enemies, and, when a crimson crystal golem was near, its owner received a boon of increased accuracy and increased chance of dodging attacks.

History
Crimson crystal golems' creation took place at the very end of the late  15 century DR. By the order of Lord Neverember, Protector's Jubilee Parade and Festival goers were given an opportunity to purchase crimson crystal items and constructs.

## Crimson crystal horse

Crimson crystal horses were a unique variety of golems created to celebrate the Protector's Jubilee, an anniversary celebration of the city's rebirth, hosted in the late 15 century DR in Neverwinter, the Jewel of the North.

Description
Crimson crystal horses were, as it was evident from the name, carved of red crystal. They emitted magical sounds that vaguely resembled the sounds of a real steed channeled through a tunnel of crystalline materials. The surface of these golems was smooth to the touch and reflected light magestically as if made out of blood-filled gemstones.

These constructs were crafted purely for ceremonial use. However, they also had some limited use in battle. Their body could be shattered in battle, dealing devastating damage to the owner's enemies, and, when a crimson crystal golem was near, its owner received a boon of increased accuracy and increased chance of dodging attacks.

History

## Crimson Tread

The Crimson Tread was a mysterious haunting that appeared on the streets of Jhaansciim and chilled the blood of inhabitants along the shores of Amn and Tethyr in the late 14 century DR.

History
The Crimson Tread manifested itself as bloody footprints that appeared behind unsuspecting villages or visitors to Jhaansciim. The crimson footprints were not caused by the villagers' bleeding, as they showed no signs of trauma. These bloody prints were the omen of looming doom and the villager's life to be taken by the Crimson Tread within a single tenday. It was unknown whether leaving Jhaanscii could save the doomed from certain death.

Some villagers claimed that the Crimson Tread was the restless spirit of Hathem Gedlaera, a troubled boy who left Jhaansciim for Athkatla and joined the Shadow Thieves. Hathem swore that one day he would pay his tormenters in Jhaansciim back. Hathem died a failure sometime before the late 14 century DR, his severed foot was found in the town of Mosstone, and since then, the Crimson Tread began claiming lives.

## Craster

Craster was a young boy living in a logging camp on the Savage Frontier during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
He was small and skinny, and also often filthy.

Personality
Craster was fond of mean humour, but didn't laugh long when it aimed at him.

Possessions
Craster and local kids used a treehouse.

Relationships
Craster was friends with three other local kids, Syndar, Kirsk, and McDodd. They hosted Nollo Rellock at their shared treehouse yearly.

History
In the, Nollo Rellock was a guest at a treehouse with four local kids at a logging camp somewhere in the Savage Frontier. The kids were familiar with his father's itinerant shows, and remembered particular exhibits for two years prior, though they hadn't forgotten he'd exaggerated on the authenticity of some of them; one of them said he'd done that thrice in three years, strongly implying that was as long as they'd met, though he also described a fourth exhibit. During the conversation, Nollo let slip that he'd seen a dragon, and in his excitement, accidentally over-shared that his father had a baby dragon caged in the barn where he kept his wagons. The other kids' disbelief only egged him on.

Ultimately, Kirsk, one of the fellow kids, offered to pay if he showed them the dragon and it was in fact a dragon. Nollo agreed to those provisions, and led them to the barn, where he pointed out the brass dragon, and offered them to feed it for an extra copper. McDodd, the meanest, played keep-away with the fish rather than feeding it to the dragon; it got a gout of dragonfire burning his hair for that. In the conmotion, a blue dragon broke into the barn, and shattered a wagon, burying Nollo under its rubble. Fortunately for him, and for McDodd and Craster, who were too out of balance to react to much, Kirsk took the wyrmling and fled out of the barn alongside Syndar; it was only interested in slaying the brass dragon.

The chase between the dragons extended from that barn to a wooden mill. In the aftermath, Beccard Rellock found his son Nollo under some collapsed wooden planks. Craster was nearby, also in a wagon.

## Crymara al Rundeen

Crymara al Rundeen was a human thief and a founder and leader of the Clenched Fist, a Rundeen-affiliated mercenary and adventuring company based in Schamedar in Calimshan in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
She was a sensual and seductive woman, but carried an explosive temper.

Abilities
She was skilled in thievery and deft conversation, was strong, and had a talent with throwing knives.

Relationships
A fellow Clenched Fist leader, the wereboar Wagym, was unwisely attracted to Crymara.

## Crossed Swords

Crossed Swords was a tavern in the Central Port of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
Crossed Swords was a one-story building in fair condition.

Services
The establishment was owned and operated by a group of professional duelists who hired out their services for combat. Their motivations ranged from defending a client's honor or settling a vendetta to pursuing trivial grievances, provided sufficient payment was offered.

The duelists' fees were scaled according to the danger of the engagement. However, they maintained strict boundaries regarding whom they challenged. Entities such as dragons, beholders, and illithids were excluded, as were the Skulls of Skullport and recognized authorities in Skullport, including high-ranking members of the Iron Ring, prominent smugglers, powerful wizards, and drow of House Tanor'Thal.

The tavern itself offered alcohol primarily to accommodate its duelists and to facilitate the negotiation and conclusion of contracts. Drink prices were high, though the selection of foreign wines was notable for both quality and variety.

Inhabitants
In 1370 DR, the leader of the duelists was Kenth.

References

## Crakadan Earthdigger

Crakadan Earthdigger was a dwarven cleric of Tempus, and an adventurer of Ravens Bluff in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
He had dark brown hair and brown eyes, with ruddy, tanned skin.

Personality
He was dour, serious, given to drink, and devoid of frivolity.

Abilities
Though a cleric, he didn't turn undead; he had instead a granted power that allowed him to enter a berserk rage. He had notable wisdom, ideal for a cleric, and equal strength and resilience.

Possessions
He had a ring of protection and a magical flail, as well as a favourite mug he carried everywhere.

Activities
By the late 14 century DR, Crakadan Earthdigger had become an adventurer in Ravens Bluff.

Relationships
Regarding his adventuring companions, he felt a bond of camaraderie with Aliana Meriadeth, and he both admired and condemned (mostly out of envy) the talents of Serena Worthwood. He went to bars together with Taldar Everwind on occassion, though he didn't approve of how dashing Taldar was. He tried not to bring up the contradictions in Tanis Obereck's life up, because he considered the mage pleasant enough. Finally, he found Akira Iko admirable, and didn't regard him fleeing his ancestral lands as a disgrace.

History
Crakadan Earthdigger left his ancestral clan to join a temple of Tempus, feeling a call to that faith; upon returning, his proselytism was not welcome, so he eventually came to Ravens Bluff, where he joined the civic temple of Tempus, and there met Akira Iko, who introduced him to the rest of his adventuring companions. Crakadan joined their party.

Appendix
Background
Crakadan originally appeared in Polyhedron #113, with only one minor difference: a different name for the god he worships, which was from a home setting in use by Robert Wiese rather than the Forgotten Realms. These characters were adapted for the Adventurer's Guild version of the Special Delivery module in 1998, and were reused for at least one more module, Legacy of Venom, in 1999.

## Crown of Salt

The Crown of Salt was an artifact from Myth Nantar.

Description
It was a slim, clear-crystal tiara made of salt crystals.

Powers
The device's powers were unknown; though it registered as magical, it displayed no powers.

History
The artifact was stolen by the pirate Vilander at some point.

In the, if the Selgauntan expedition captained by Sturge Whitsunday made contact with Myth Nantar, they'd likely find themselves charged with recovering the Crown from Vilander's hideout in Agenais. If they succeeded, Selgaunt would have a way to call on aid from Myth Nantar, just in time for elections, too, and the crown would be worn by Tu'ola'sara hence.

Notable Owners
 Vilander
